AP Calculus AB - Unit 6 Canonical Content Bank

Integration and Accumulation | Sections 6.1-6.5 | Canonical destination-first source

1. Notes Example / You Try It

Section 6.1 - Accumulation and Area

U6-S1-NOTE-EX01
Section 6.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The figure shows a piecewise-linear rate $R(t)$ in liters per minute for $0\le t\le 6$. Find the net accumulated change $\int_0^6 R(t)\,dt$ and state its units.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $-5$ liters
Solution / scoring notes: Because each segment is linear, add the signed trapezoid areas over $[0,2]$, $[2,4]$, and $[4,6]$.
U6-S1-NOTE-YTI01
Section 6.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

Given $\int_1^4 f(x)\,dx=8$ and $\int_4^7 f(x)\,dx=-5$, evaluate $\int_1^7 f(x)\,dx$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use additivity of definite integrals across adjacent intervals.
U6-S1-NOTE-EX02
Section 6.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Given $\int_1^4 f(x)\,dx=5$ and $\int_4^7 f(x)\,dx=3$, evaluate $\int_1^7 f(x)\,dx$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $8$
Solution / scoring notes: Use additivity of definite integrals across adjacent intervals.
U6-S1-NOTE-YTI02
Section 6.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

On an interval of width 2, a rate graph forms a triangle of height 5 entirely above the horizontal axis. What is the signed contribution to the definite integral?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $5$
Solution / scoring notes: Triangle area is one-half base times height, and regions below the axis contribute negatively.
U6-S1-NOTE-EX03
Section 6.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

On an interval of width 6, a rate graph forms a triangle of height 5 entirely below the horizontal axis. What is the signed contribution to the definite integral?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $-15$
Solution / scoring notes: Triangle area is one-half base times height, and regions below the axis contribute negatively.
U6-S1-NOTE-YTI03
Section 6.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A tank initially contains 35 liters. The net change over the next hour is $\int_0^1 R(t)\,dt=-12$ liters. How much is in the tank after one hour?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: 23 liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount equals initial amount plus accumulated change.
U6-S1-NOTE-EX04
Section 6.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A tank initially contains 20 liters. The net change over the next hour is $\int_0^1 R(t)\,dt=-12$ liters. How much is in the tank after one hour?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: 8 liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount equals initial amount plus accumulated change.
U6-S1-NOTE-YTI04
Section 6.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

A rate $r(t)$ is measured in people per hour. State the units of $\int_a^b r(t)\,dt$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: people
Solution / scoring notes: Integrating a rate multiplies by the input-time unit, leaving accumulated output units.

Section 6.2 - Riemann Sums

U6-S2-NOTE-EX01
Section 6.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use a left Riemann sum on the four equal subintervals to approximate $\int_0^4 f(x)\,dx$.
$x$$0$$1$$2$$3$$4$
$f(x)$$4$$3$$6$$7$$8$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $20$
Solution / scoring notes: Each width is 1; multiply each selected endpoint value by its width and add.
U6-S2-NOTE-YTI01
Section 6.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Use a trapezoidal sum on the nonuniform partition shown to approximate the integral from 0 to 6.
$x$$0$$1$$3$$6$
$f(x)$$1$$2$$7$$6$

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $30$
Solution / scoring notes: Use each actual subinterval width; do not assume the partition is uniform.
U6-S2-NOTE-EX02
Section 6.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use a trapezoidal sum on the nonuniform partition shown to approximate the integral from 0 to 6.
$x$$0$$1$$3$$6$
$f(x)$$1$$2$$3$$6$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $20$
Solution / scoring notes: Use each actual subinterval width; do not assume the partition is uniform.
U6-S2-NOTE-YTI02
Section 6.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Use a midpoint sum with 4 equal subintervals to approximate $\int_{1}^{5} (x^{2} + 1)\,dx$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $45$
Solution / scoring notes: The width is 1; evaluate the function at the four subinterval midpoints and add.
U6-S2-NOTE-EX03
Section 6.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use a midpoint sum with 4 equal subintervals to approximate $\int_{0}^{4} (x^{2} + 1)\,dx$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $25$
Solution / scoring notes: The width is 1; evaluate the function at the four subinterval midpoints and add.
U6-S2-NOTE-YTI03
Section 6.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Write as a definite integral: $\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^n \left(5/n\right)\left[(0+5k/n)^2+1\right]$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\int_{0}^{5}(x^2+1)\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: The factor $5/n$ is $\Delta x$ and the sample point is a right endpoint across an interval of length 5.
U6-S2-NOTE-EX04
Section 6.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Write as a definite integral: $\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^n \left(5/n\right)\left[(1+5k/n)^2+1\right]$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\int_{1}^{6}(x^2+1)\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: The factor $5/n$ is $\Delta x$ and the sample point is a right endpoint across an interval of length 5.
U6-S2-NOTE-YTI04
Section 6.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

If $f$ is positive and decreasing on $[a,b]$, does a left Riemann sum give an overestimate or underestimate of the integral?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: overestimate
Solution / scoring notes: Compare each rectangle height with function values on the same subinterval.

Section 6.3 - FTC Part 1 and Accumulation Functions

U6-S3-NOTE-EX01
Section 6.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Let $G(x)=\int_{0}^{x^{2} + 1}(t^2+1)\,dt$. Find $G'(x)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $2 x \left(x^{4} + 2 x^{2} + 2\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 gives the integrand evaluated at the upper limit, multiplied by the derivative of that upper limit.
U6-S3-NOTE-YTI01
Section 6.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The figure shows $f$. For $A(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, state where $A$ is increasing.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $(-\infty,-3.999)$ and $(1.999,\infty)$
Solution / scoring notes: Because $A'(x)=f(x)$, the accumulation function increases exactly where the integrand is positive.
U6-S3-NOTE-EX02
Section 6.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The figure shows $f$. For $A(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, state where $A$ is increasing.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $(-\infty,2.002)$ and $(3.998,\infty)$
Solution / scoring notes: Because $A'(x)=f(x)$, the accumulation function increases exactly where the integrand is positive.
U6-S3-NOTE-YTI02
Section 6.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

If $H(x)=\int_2^x (t^{3} - 2 t + 1)\,dt$, find $H'(x)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x^{3} - 2 x + 1$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 returns the integrand evaluated at the variable upper limit.
U6-S3-NOTE-EX03
Section 6.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

If $H(x)=\int_2^x (t^{3} - 3 t + 1)\,dt$, find $H'(x)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x^{3} - 3 x + 1$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 returns the integrand evaluated at the variable upper limit.
U6-S3-NOTE-YTI03
Section 6.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

If $K(x)=\int_1^x (\sin t+t^2)\,dt$, find $K''(x)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\cos x+2x$
Solution / scoring notes: $K'(x)=\sin x+x^2$ by FTC, then differentiate once more.
U6-S3-NOTE-EX04
Section 6.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

If $K(x)=\int_1^x (\sin t+t^2)\,dt$, find $K''(x)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\cos x+2x$
Solution / scoring notes: $K'(x)=\sin x+x^2$ by FTC, then differentiate once more.
U6-S3-NOTE-YTI04
Section 6.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

An accumulation function is $F(x)=\int_{2}^x g(t)\,dt$. What is $F(2)$, regardless of $g$?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: An integral with identical lower and upper bounds is zero.

Section 6.4 - FTC Part 2 and Antiderivatives

U6-S4-NOTE-EX01
Section 6.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Find the general antiderivative of $2 x \left(x^{2} - 1\right)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{x^{4}}{2} - x^{2}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate term by term and include an arbitrary constant.
U6-S4-NOTE-YTI01
Section 6.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Evaluate $\int_{0}^{3} (2 x^{2} - x + 1)\,dx$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{33}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Find an antiderivative and compute upper-value minus lower-value.
U6-S4-NOTE-EX02
Section 6.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Evaluate $\int_{0}^{1} (2 x^{2} - 3 x + 4)\,dx$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{19}{6}$
Solution / scoring notes: Find an antiderivative and compute upper-value minus lower-value.
U6-S4-NOTE-YTI02
Section 6.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

Which expression correctly distinguishes an indefinite integral from a definite integral: $\int f(x)\,dx$ or $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$? State where $C$ belongs.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The indefinite integral represents an antiderivative family and includes $C$; the definite integral is a number and does not receive $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: Constants of integration belong to antiderivative families, not evaluated definite integrals.
U6-S4-NOTE-EX03
Section 6.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Which expression correctly distinguishes an indefinite integral from a definite integral: $\int f(x)\,dx$ or $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$? State where $C$ belongs.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: The indefinite integral represents an antiderivative family and includes $C$; the definite integral is a number and does not receive $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: Constants of integration belong to antiderivative families, not evaluated definite integrals.
U6-S4-NOTE-YTI03
Section 6.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

If $\int_{-3}^{-2} f(x)\,dx=4$, evaluate $\int_{-2}^{-3} f(x)\,dx$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-4$
Solution / scoring notes: Reversing the bounds changes the sign of a definite integral.
U6-S4-NOTE-EX04
Section 6.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

If $\int_{2}^{3} f(x)\,dx=-8$, evaluate $\int_{3}^{2} f(x)\,dx$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $8$
Solution / scoring notes: Reversing the bounds changes the sign of a definite integral.
U6-S4-NOTE-YTI04
Section 6.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find the particular antiderivative $F$ satisfying $F'(x)=3 x^{2} - 4 x + 2$ and $F(0)=5$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $F(x)=x^{3} - 2 x^{2} + 2 x + 5$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate, include $C$, then use the initial condition to determine the constant.

Section 6.5 - u-Substitution and Strategy

U6-S5-NOTE-EX01
Section 6.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Evaluate $\int 2 \left(2 x - 3\right)^{2}\,dx$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{\left(2 x - 3\right)^{3}}{3}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Let $u=2 x - 3$; then $du=2\,dx$, so the integral becomes a power of $u$.
U6-S5-NOTE-YTI01
Section 6.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Evaluate $\int_0^{2} 2 x \left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}\,dx$ using a change of variable.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{208}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $u=x^2+2$ and change the bounds or back-substitute consistently.
U6-S5-NOTE-EX02
Section 6.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Evaluate $\int_0^{2} 2 x \left(x^{2} + 4\right)^{2}\,dx$ using a change of variable.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{448}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $u=x^2+4$ and change the bounds or back-substitute consistently.
U6-S5-NOTE-YTI02
Section 6.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Choose an efficient preparation and evaluate $\int \frac{x^3+2x^2-x}{x}\,dx$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x \left(\frac{x^{2}}{3} + x - 1\right)+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel/divide termwise first; substitution is unnecessary after simplification.
U6-S5-NOTE-EX03
Section 6.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Choose an efficient preparation and evaluate $\int \frac{x^3+2x^2-x}{x}\,dx$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x \left(\frac{x^{2}}{3} + x - 1\right)+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel/divide termwise first; substitution is unnecessary after simplification.
U6-S5-NOTE-YTI03
Section 6.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.
U6-S5-NOTE-EX04
Section 6.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.
U6-S5-NOTE-YTI04
Section 6.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Evaluate $\int \frac{1}{x^{2} + 2 x + 2}\,dx$ by preparing the quadratic denominator.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\operatorname{atan}{\left(x + 1 \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Complete the square to create a shifted $a^2+u^2$ form, then use the inverse-tangent antiderivative.

Section 6.1 - Accumulation and Area

Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Given $\int_1^4 f(x)\,dx=8$ and $\int_4^7 f(x)\,dx=-5$, evaluate $\int_1^7 f(x)\,dx$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use additivity of definite integrals across adjacent intervals.
U6-S1-PS1-C02
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
On an interval of width 2, a rate graph forms a triangle of height 3 entirely above the horizontal axis. What is the signed contribution to the definite integral?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Triangle area is one-half base times height, and regions below the axis contribute negatively.
U6-S1-PS1-C03
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A tank initially contains 50 liters. The net change over the next hour is $\int_0^1 R(t)\,dt=-7$ liters. How much is in the tank after one hour?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: 43 liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount equals initial amount plus accumulated change.
U6-S1-PS1-C04
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
A rate $r(t)$ is measured in liters per minute. State the units of $\int_a^b r(t)\,dt$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: liters
Solution / scoring notes: Integrating a rate multiplies by the input-time unit, leaving accumulated output units.
U6-S1-PS1-C05
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
A student adds the absolute geometric areas of every region between a rate graph and the axis to compute a definite integral. Explain the error and state when absolute areas would instead be appropriate.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: A definite integral uses signed area; regions below the axis subtract. Absolute areas are used when the requested quantity is total accumulated magnitude rather than net change.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign of the integrand controls signed accumulation.
U6-S1-PS1-L01
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Two positive numbers have sum 30. Find the value of $x$ that maximizes $P(x)=x(30-x)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $15$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate the downward-opening quadratic and check the feasible interval.
U6-S1-PS1-L02
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A rectangle has perimeter 64. Write a one-variable area objective if one side is $x$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $A(x)=x(32-x)$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the perimeter constraint to express the second side.
U6-S1-PS1-L03
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 3
Why is finding one critical point not by itself a complete justification of an absolute optimum on a closed feasible interval?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Endpoints and all admissible critical candidates must be compared, and the model/domain must be valid.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization combines a correct model, feasible domain, and extremum evidence.
U6-S1-PS1-L04
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A three-sided rectangular pen uses 84 m of fencing with one side along a wall. If each perpendicular side is $x$, find the maximizing $x$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $21$
Solution / scoring notes: The objective is $A(x)=x(84-2x)$; set its derivative to zero and verify the feasible maximum.
U6-S1-PS1-L05
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
Distinguish the constraint from the objective function in an optimization model.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The constraint restricts feasible quantities; the objective is the quantity to maximize or minimize.
Solution / scoring notes: They play different mathematical roles.
U6-S1-PS1-M01
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to 3}\frac{x^2-9}{x-3}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $6$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor, cancel on the punctured neighborhood, and substitute.
U6-S1-PS1-M02

Find $f'(x)$ for $f(x)=\left(3 x - 4\right) \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $9 x^{2} - 8 x + 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule and simplify.

Solve $4(x-1)=3x+4$ for $x$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $8$
Solution / scoring notes: Expand, collect the x-terms, and isolate the variable.
U6-S1-PS1-M05
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
A differentiable function satisfies $F(3)=8$ and $F'(3)=-2$. Interpret the derivative in a complete rate statement.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Near input 3, the output is decreasing at about 2 output-units per input-unit.
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative is an instantaneous rate of change.
U6-S1-PS1-M06
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
A graph of $h'$ lies above the x-axis on $(1,4)$. What does this imply about $h$ there?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $h$ is increasing on $(1,4)$.
Solution / scoring notes: A positive first derivative means the function is increasing.
U6-S1-PS1-M07
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $g(x)=\left(4 x - 5\right)^{3}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $g'(x)=12 \left(4 x - 5\right)^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the chain rule and simplify.
U6-S1-PS1-M08
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
A quantity $Q(t)$ is measured in liters and time in minutes. State the units of $Q'(t)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: liters per minutes
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative has output units divided by input units.
U6-S1-PS1-M09

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to 4}\frac{x^2-16}{x-4}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $8$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor, cancel on the punctured neighborhood, and substitute.
U6-S1-PS1-M10

Find $f'(x)$ for $f(x)=\left(2 x + 3\right) \left(x^{2} + 3\right)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $6 x^{2} + 6 x + 6$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule and simplify.

Section 6.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
A student uses a single common width in a Riemann sum even though the table partition is nonuniform. Critique the setup and state the correct structural form.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\sum f(x_i^*)\Delta x_i$
Solution / scoring notes: Each subinterval must use its own width $\Delta x_i$ when the partition is nonuniform.

Answer: $F(3)=\int_0^3 f(t)\,dt$ while $f(3)$ is the integrand value at one input.

U6-S4-PS1-C02
Section 6.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Which expression correctly distinguishes an indefinite integral from a definite integral: $\int f(x)\,dx$ or $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$? State where $C$ belongs.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The indefinite integral represents an antiderivative family and includes $C$; the definite integral is a number and does not receive $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: Constants of integration belong to antiderivative families, not evaluated definite integrals.

Section 6.5 - u-Substitution and Strategy

U6-S5-PS1-C01
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int_0^{1} 2 x \left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}\,dx$ using a change of variable.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{19}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $u=x^2+2$ and change the bounds or back-substitute consistently.
U6-S5-PS1-C02
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Choose an efficient preparation and evaluate $\int \frac{x^3+2x^2-x}{x}\,dx$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $x \left(\frac{x^{2}}{3} + x - 1\right)+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel/divide termwise first; substitution is unnecessary after simplification.
U6-S5-PS1-C03
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.
U6-S5-PS1-C04
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{1}{x^{2} + 6 x + 18}\,dx$ by preparing the quadratic denominator.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{\operatorname{atan}{\left(\frac{x}{3} + 1 \right)}}{3}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Complete the square to create a shifted $a^2+u^2$ form, then use the inverse-tangent antiderivative.
U6-S5-PS1-C05
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
For the integrand $\frac{x^3+1}{x^2+1}$, compare substitution with algebraic division as first strategies. Choose the more efficient first move and justify it.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Polynomial division is the better first move because the numerator degree is at least the denominator degree; it exposes a simpler rational remainder.
Solution / scoring notes: Strategic integration begins by reading the integrand structure rather than automatically choosing $u$.

Practice Set 2

Section 6.1 - Accumulation and Area

U6-S1-PS2-C01
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
On an interval of width 2, a rate graph forms a triangle of height 2 entirely above the horizontal axis. What is the signed contribution to the definite integral?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Triangle area is one-half base times height, and regions below the axis contribute negatively.
U6-S1-PS2-C02
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
A tank initially contains 50 liters. The net change over the next hour is $\int_0^1 R(t)\,dt=8$ liters. How much is in the tank after one hour?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: 58 liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount equals initial amount plus accumulated change.
U6-S1-PS2-C03
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
A rate $r(t)$ is measured in people per hour. State the units of $\int_a^b r(t)\,dt$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: people
Solution / scoring notes: Integrating a rate multiplies by the input-time unit, leaving accumulated output units.
U6-S1-PS2-C04
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student adds the absolute geometric areas of every region between a rate graph and the axis to compute a definite integral. Explain the error and state when absolute areas would instead be appropriate.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: A definite integral uses signed area; regions below the axis subtract. Absolute areas are used when the requested quantity is total accumulated magnitude rather than net change.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign of the integrand controls signed accumulation.
U6-S1-PS2-C05
Section 6.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-9$ liters
Solution / scoring notes: Because each segment is linear, add the signed trapezoid areas over $[0,2]$, $[2,4]$, and $[4,6]$.

Why is finding one critical point not by itself a complete justification of an absolute optimum on a closed feasible interval?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Endpoints and all admissible critical candidates must be compared, and the model/domain must be valid.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization combines a correct model, feasible domain, and extremum evidence.

Answer: $21$
Solution / scoring notes: The objective is $A(x)=x(84-2x)$; set its derivative to zero and verify the feasible maximum.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The constraint restricts feasible quantities; the objective is the quantity to maximize or minimize.
Solution / scoring notes: They play different mathematical roles.

U6-S3-PS2-C02
Section 6.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
If $K(x)=\int_1^x (\sin t+t^2)\,dt$, find $K''(x)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\cos x+2x$
Solution / scoring notes: $K'(x)=\sin x+x^2$ by FTC, then differentiate once more.
U6-S3-PS2-C03
Section 6.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
An accumulation function is $F(x)=\int_{4}^x g(t)\,dt$. What is $F(4)$, regardless of $g$?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: An integral with identical lower and upper bounds is zero.
U6-S3-PS2-C04
Section 6.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student says that for $F(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, the value $F(3)$ must equal $f(3)$. Explain why this confuses two different quantities.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $F(3)=\int_0^3 f(t)\,dt$ while $f(3)$ is the integrand value at one input.
Solution / scoring notes: FTC relates $F'(x)$ to $f(x)$; it does not generally equate the accumulation value with the integrand value.

Answer: $2 x \left(x^{4} + 2 x^{2} + 2\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 gives the integrand evaluated at the upper limit, multiplied by the derivative of that upper limit.

Section 6.4 - FTC Part 2 and Antiderivatives

U6-S4-PS2-C01
Section 6.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
Which expression correctly distinguishes an indefinite integral from a definite integral: $\int f(x)\,dx$ or $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$? State where $C$ belongs.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The indefinite integral represents an antiderivative family and includes $C$; the definite integral is a number and does not receive $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: Constants of integration belong to antiderivative families, not evaluated definite integrals.
U6-S4-PS2-C02
Section 6.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
If $\int_{-3}^{-2} f(x)\,dx=7$, evaluate $\int_{-2}^{-3} f(x)\,dx$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-7$
Solution / scoring notes: Reversing the bounds changes the sign of a definite integral.
U6-S4-PS2-C03
Section 6.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Find the particular antiderivative $F$ satisfying $F'(x)=3 x^{2} - 4 x + 2$ and $F(2)=1$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $F(x)=x^{3} - 2 x^{2} + 2 x - 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate, include $C$, then use the initial condition to determine the constant.
U6-S4-PS2-C04
Section 6.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student writes $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)+C$. Critique the notation and repair the statement.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)$
Solution / scoring notes: The constant cancels in a definite-integral evaluation and should not be appended to the numerical result.
U6-S4-PS2-C05
Section 6.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
Find the general antiderivative of $x \left(3 x^{2} - 2\right)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{3 x^{4}}{4} - x^{2}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate term by term and include an arbitrary constant.

Solution / scoring notes: FTC relates $F'(x)$ to $f(x)$; it does not generally equate the accumulation value with the integrand value.

Section 6.5 - u-Substitution and Strategy

U6-S5-PS2-C01
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Choose an efficient preparation and evaluate $\int \frac{x^3+2x^2-x}{x}\,dx$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $x \left(\frac{x^{2}}{3} + x - 1\right)+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel/divide termwise first; substitution is unnecessary after simplification.
U6-S5-PS2-C02
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.
U6-S5-PS2-C03
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{1}{x^{2} + 2 x + 2}\,dx$ by preparing the quadratic denominator.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\operatorname{atan}{\left(x + 1 \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Complete the square to create a shifted $a^2+u^2$ form, then use the inverse-tangent antiderivative.
U6-S5-PS2-C04
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
For the integrand $\frac{x^3+1}{x^2+1}$, compare substitution with algebraic division as first strategies. Choose the more efficient first move and justify it.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Polynomial division is the better first move because the numerator degree is at least the denominator degree; it exposes a simpler rational remainder.
Solution / scoring notes: Strategic integration begins by reading the integrand structure rather than automatically choosing $u$.
U6-S5-PS2-C05
Section 6.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int 2 \left(2 x - 3\right)^{2}\,dx$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{\left(2 x - 3\right)^{3}}{3}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Let $u=2 x - 3$; then $du=2\,dx$, so the integral becomes a power of $u$.
U6-S5-PS2-L01

A student writes $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)+C$. Critique the notation and repair the statement.

Make the concluding notation unambiguous about the point being discussed.

Answer: $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)$
Solution / scoring notes: The constant cancels in a definite-integral evaluation and should not be appended to the numerical result.

Extra Practice

Section 6.1 - Accumulation and Area

U6-S1-XP-C01
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
A rate $r(t)$ is measured in liters per minute. State the units of $\int_a^b r(t)\,dt$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: liters
Solution / scoring notes: Integrating a rate multiplies by the input-time unit, leaving accumulated output units.
U6-S1-XP-C02
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student adds the absolute geometric areas of every region between a rate graph and the axis to compute a definite integral. Explain the error and state when absolute areas would instead be appropriate.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: A definite integral uses signed area; regions below the axis subtract. Absolute areas are used when the requested quantity is total accumulated magnitude rather than net change.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign of the integrand controls signed accumulation.
U6-S1-XP-C03
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The figure shows a piecewise-linear rate $R(t)$ in liters per minute for $0\le t\le 6$. Find the net accumulated change $\int_0^6 R(t)\,dt$ and state its units.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $18$ liters
Solution / scoring notes: Because each segment is linear, add the signed trapezoid areas over $[0,2]$, $[2,4]$, and $[4,6]$.
U6-S1-XP-C04
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
Given $\int_1^4 f(x)\,dx=8$ and $\int_4^7 f(x)\,dx=-5$, evaluate $\int_1^7 f(x)\,dx$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use additivity of definite integrals across adjacent intervals.
U6-S1-XP-C05
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
On an interval of width 6, a rate graph forms a triangle of height 5 entirely below the horizontal axis. What is the signed contribution to the definite integral?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $-15$
Solution / scoring notes: Triangle area is one-half base times height, and regions below the axis contribute negatively.
U6-S1-XP-L01
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A three-sided rectangular pen uses 84 m of fencing with one side along a wall. If each perpendicular side is $x$, find the maximizing $x$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $21$
Solution / scoring notes: The objective is $A(x)=x(84-2x)$; set its derivative to zero and verify the feasible maximum.
U6-S1-XP-L02
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 1
Distinguish the constraint from the objective function in an optimization model.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The constraint restricts feasible quantities; the objective is the quantity to maximize or minimize.
Solution / scoring notes: They play different mathematical roles.
U6-S1-XP-L03
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
Two positive numbers have sum 36. Find the value of $x$ that maximizes $P(x)=x(36-x)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $18$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate the downward-opening quadratic and check the feasible interval.
U6-S1-XP-L04
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A rectangle has perimeter 56. Write a one-variable area objective if one side is $x$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $A(x)=x(28-x)$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the perimeter constraint to express the second side.
U6-S1-XP-L05
Section 6.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 3
Why is finding one critical point not by itself a complete justification of an absolute optimum on a closed feasible interval?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Endpoints and all admissible critical candidates must be compared, and the model/domain must be valid.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization combines a correct model, feasible domain, and extremum evidence.

Section 6.2 - Riemann Sums

U6-S2-XP-C01
Section 6.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
If $f$ is positive and increasing on $[a,b]$, does a left Riemann sum give an overestimate or underestimate of the integral?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: underestimate
Solution / scoring notes: Compare each rectangle height with function values on the same subinterval.
U6-S2-XP-C02
Section 6.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student uses a single common width in a Riemann sum even though the table partition is nonuniform. Critique the setup and state the correct structural form.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\sum f(x_i^*)\Delta x_i$
Solution / scoring notes: Each subinterval must use its own width $\Delta x_i$ when the partition is nonuniform.
U6-S2-XP-C03
Section 6.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Use a right Riemann sum on the four equal subintervals to approximate $\int_0^4 f(x)\,dx$.
$x$$0$$1$$2$$3$$4$
$f(x)$$1$$3$$6$$7$$4$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $20$
Solution / scoring notes: Each width is 1; multiply each selected endpoint value by its width and add.
U6-S2-XP-C04
Section 6.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Use a trapezoidal sum on the nonuniform partition shown to approximate the integral from 0 to 6.
$x$$0$$1$$3$$6$
$f(x)$$2$$2$$5$$4$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{45}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use each actual subinterval width; do not assume the partition is uniform.
U6-S2-XP-C05
Section 6.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Use a midpoint sum with 4 equal subintervals to approximate $\int_{1}^{5} (x^{2} + 2)\,dx$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $49$
Solution / scoring notes: The width is 1; evaluate the function at the four subinterval midpoints and add.
U6-S2-XP-L01

Given $\int_1^4 f(x)\,dx=6$ and $\int_4^7 f(x)\,dx=-2$, evaluate $\int_1^7 f(x)\,dx$.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Triangle area is one-half base times height, and regions below the axis contribute negatively.

Solution / scoring notes: Final amount equals initial amount plus accumulated change.

U6-S2-XP-L05

A student adds the absolute geometric areas of every region between a rate graph and the axis to compute a definite integral. Explain the error and state when absolute areas would instead be appropriate.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Keep any supporting work directly tied to the requested quantity.

Answer: A definite integral uses signed area; regions below the axis subtract. Absolute areas are used when the requested quantity is total accumulated magnitude rather than net change.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign of the integrand controls signed accumulation.

Section 6.3 - FTC Part 1 and Accumulation Functions

U6-S3-XP-C01
Section 6.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
An accumulation function is $F(x)=\int_{3}^x g(t)\,dt$. What is $F(3)$, regardless of $g$?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: An integral with identical lower and upper bounds is zero.
U6-S3-XP-C02
Section 6.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student says that for $F(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, the value $F(3)$ must equal $f(3)$. Explain why this confuses two different quantities.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $F(3)=\int_0^3 f(t)\,dt$ while $f(3)$ is the integrand value at one input.
Solution / scoring notes: FTC relates $F'(x)$ to $f(x)$; it does not generally equate the accumulation value with the integrand value.
U6-S3-XP-C03
Section 6.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Let $G(x)=\int_{0}^{x^{3} + 1}(t^2+1)\,dt$. Find $G'(x)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $3 x^{2} \left(x^{6} + 2 x^{3} + 2\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 gives the integrand evaluated at the upper limit, multiplied by the derivative of that upper limit.
U6-S3-XP-C04
Section 6.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The figure shows $f$. For $A(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, state where $A$ is increasing.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $(2.991,4.009)$
Solution / scoring notes: Because $A'(x)=f(x)$, the accumulation function increases exactly where the integrand is positive.

If $H(x)=\int_2^x (t^{3} - 2 t + 1)\,dt$, find $H'(x)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $x^{3} - 2 x + 1$

Section 6.4 - FTC Part 2 and Antiderivatives

U6-S4-XP-C01
Section 6.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Find the particular antiderivative $F$ satisfying $F'(x)=3 x^{2} - 4 x + 2$ and $F(2)=1$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $F(x)=x^{3} - 2 x^{2} + 2 x - 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate, include $C$, then use the initial condition to determine the constant.
U6-S4-XP-C02
Section 6.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student writes $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)+C$. Critique the notation and repair the statement.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)$
Solution / scoring notes: The constant cancels in a definite-integral evaluation and should not be appended to the numerical result.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{5 x^{4}}{4} - x^{2}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate term by term and include an arbitrary constant.

U6-S4-XP-C05

Which expression correctly distinguishes an indefinite integral from a definite integral: $\int f(x)\,dx$ or $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$? State where $C$ belongs.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The indefinite integral represents an antiderivative family and includes $C$; the definite integral is a number and does not receive $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: Constants of integration belong to antiderivative families, not evaluated definite integrals.

Section 6.5 - u-Substitution and Strategy

U6-S5-XP-C01
Section 6.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{1}{x^{2} + 6 x + 13}\,dx$ by preparing the quadratic denominator.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{\operatorname{atan}{\left(\frac{x}{2} + \frac{3}{2} \right)}}{2}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Complete the square to create a shifted $a^2+u^2$ form, then use the inverse-tangent antiderivative.
U6-S5-XP-C02
Section 6.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
For the integrand $\frac{x^3+1}{x^2+1}$, compare substitution with algebraic division as first strategies. Choose the more efficient first move and justify it.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Polynomial division is the better first move because the numerator degree is at least the denominator degree; it exposes a simpler rational remainder.
Solution / scoring notes: Strategic integration begins by reading the integrand structure rather than automatically choosing $u$.
U6-S5-XP-C03
Section 6.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int 2 \left(2 x + 1\right)^{3}\,dx$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{\left(2 x + 1\right)^{4}}{4}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Let $u=2 x + 1$; then $du=2\,dx$, so the integral becomes a power of $u$.
U6-S5-XP-C04
Section 6.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{19}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $u=x^2+2$ and change the bounds or back-substitute consistently.
U6-S5-XP-C05
Section 6.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $x \left(\frac{x^{2}}{3} + x - 1\right)+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel/divide termwise first; substitution is unnecessary after simplification.

Section 6.5 - u-Substitution and Strategy

A quantity $Q(t)$ is measured in liters and time in minutes. State the units of $Q'(t)$.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Preserve the input value or interval in the concluding statement.

Answer: liters per minutes
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative has output units divided by input units.
U6-S5-WU2-C01
Section 6.5 | Warm Up 2 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{1}{x^{2} + 4 x + 5}\,dx$ by preparing the quadratic denominator.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $\operatorname{atan}{\left(x + 2 \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Complete the square to create a shifted $a^2+u^2$ form, then use the inverse-tangent antiderivative.
U6-S5-WU2-L01
Section 6.5 | Warm Up 2 | Last | DOK 1
Which expression correctly distinguishes an indefinite integral from a definite integral: $\int f(x)\,dx$ or $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$? State where $C$ belongs.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: The indefinite integral represents an antiderivative family and includes $C$; the definite integral is a number and does not receive $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: Constants of integration belong to antiderivative families, not evaluated definite integrals.
U6-S5-WU2-M01
Section 6.5 | Warm Up 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
A differentiable function satisfies $F(3)=8$ and $F'(3)=-2$. Interpret the derivative in a complete rate statement.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Keep the final response to one clear mathematical statement.

Answer: Near input 3, the output is decreasing at about 2 output-units per input-unit.
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative is an instantaneous rate of change.
U6-S5-WU3-C01
Section 6.5 | Warm Up 3 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.
U6-S5-WU3-L01
Section 6.5 | Warm Up 3 | Last | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int_{2}^{5} (3 x^{2} - 3 x + 4)\,dx$.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: $\frac{195}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Find an antiderivative and compute upper-value minus lower-value.
U6-S5-WU3-M01
Section 6.5 | Warm Up 3 | Mastery | DOK 2
Find $f'(x)$ for $f(x)=2 \left(x - 2\right) \left(x^{2} + 3\right)$.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Make the concluding notation unambiguous about the point being discussed.

Answer: $6 x^{2} - 8 x + 6$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule and simplify.

8. Exit Tickets

Section 6.1 - Accumulation and Area

U6-S1-ET-A-C01
Section 6.1 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 1
A rate $r(t)$ is measured in meters per second. State the units of $\int_a^b r(t)\,dt$.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: meters
Solution / scoring notes: Integrating a rate multiplies by the input-time unit, leaving accumulated output units.
U6-S1-ET-A-C02
Section 6.1 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 3
A student adds the absolute geometric areas of every region between a rate graph and the axis to compute a definite integral. Explain the error and state when absolute areas would instead be appropriate.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: A definite integral uses signed area; regions below the axis subtract. Absolute areas are used when the requested quantity is total accumulated magnitude rather than net change.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign of the integrand controls signed accumulation.
U6-S1-ET-A-L01
Section 6.1 | Exit Ticket A | Last | DOK 3
Why is finding one critical point not by itself a complete justification of an absolute optimum on a closed feasible interval?

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: Endpoints and all admissible critical candidates must be compared, and the model/domain must be valid.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization combines a correct model, feasible domain, and extremum evidence.
U6-S1-ET-A-M01

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $6 x^{2} - 4 x + 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule and simplify.

Solution / scoring notes: Each subinterval must use its own width $\Delta x_i$ when the partition is nonuniform.

Section 6.3 - FTC Part 1 and Accumulation Functions

U6-S3-ET-A-C01
Section 6.3 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 1
An accumulation function is $F(x)=\int_{2}^x g(t)\,dt$. What is $F(2)$, regardless of $g$?

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: An integral with identical lower and upper bounds is zero.
U6-S3-ET-A-C02
Section 6.3 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 3
A student says that for $F(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, the value $F(3)$ must equal $f(3)$. Explain why this confuses two different quantities.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $F(3)=\int_0^3 f(t)\,dt$ while $f(3)$ is the integrand value at one input.
Solution / scoring notes: FTC relates $F'(x)$ to $f(x)$; it does not generally equate the accumulation value with the integrand value.

Answer: $F(x)=x^{3} - 2 x^{2} + 2 x + 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate, include $C$, then use the initial condition to determine the constant.

Answer: $\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx=F(2)-F(0)$
Solution / scoring notes: The constant cancels in a definite-integral evaluation and should not be appended to the numerical result.

U6-S5-ET-A-C02

For the integrand $\frac{x^3+1}{x^2+1}$, compare substitution with algebraic division as first strategies. Choose the more efficient first move and justify it.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: Polynomial division is the better first move because the numerator degree is at least the denominator degree; it exposes a simpler rational remainder.
Solution / scoring notes: Strategic integration begins by reading the integrand structure rather than automatically choosing $u$.

9. Performance / Future Tasks

Section 6.1 - Accumulation and Area

U6-S1-PERF01
Section 6.1 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

A pump rate in liters per minute is recorded every 2 minutes:
$t$$0$$2$$4$$6$$8$
$R(t)$$3$$5$$-2$$-4$$1$
. Use at least two reasonable numerical/geometric accumulation models to estimate the net volume change from 0 to 8 minutes. Compare the models, explain how negative rates affect the result, and revise your conclusion if one model treats signed change incorrectly.

Support each conclusion with the numerical, graphical, FTC, or strategy evidence requested.

Expected evidence: A complete response computes two defensible approximations, preserves signed contributions, compares them, and gives net change in liters.
Scoring criteria: Evidence must include two accumulation models, signed-area reasoning, units, a comparison, and an explicit correction/revision.
Estimated student time: 20-25 minutes
Answer: A complete response computes two defensible approximations, preserves signed contributions, compares them, and gives net change in liters.
Solution / scoring notes: Evidence must include two accumulation models, signed-area reasoning, units, a comparison, and an explicit correction/revision.

Section 6.2 - Riemann Sums

U6-S2-PERF01
Section 6.2 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

For $f(x)=x^2+1$ on $[0,4]$, compute left, right, midpoint, and trapezoid approximations using 4 equal subintervals. Rank the four approximations relative to the exact integral, formulate a claim about which methods over- or underestimate for this increasing concave-up function, then test your claim on $g(x)=5-x^2/4$ on $[0,4]$ and revise any claim that does not survive the second case.

Support each conclusion with the numerical, graphical, FTC, or strategy evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response should calculate all requested sums, compare them with exact integrals, and refine claims so monotonicity and concavity are distinguished.
Scoring criteria: Scoring requires correct computations, evidence-based comparison, a tested generalization, and a revision informed by the counterexample.
Estimated student time: 25-30 minutes
Answer: The response should calculate all requested sums, compare them with exact integrals, and refine claims so monotonicity and concavity are distinguished.
Solution / scoring notes: Scoring requires correct computations, evidence-based comparison, a tested generalization, and a revision informed by the counterexample.

Section 6.3 - FTC Part 1 and Accumulation Functions

Performance / Future Task

Let $f(x)=x^2-4$ and $A(x)=\int_{-1}^{x} f(t)\,dt$. Construct a linked representation set that includes: a sketch of $f$, intervals where $A$ increases/decreases, all critical points of $A$, the sign of $A''$ where it can be determined, and a symbolic formula for $A(x)$. Test each graphical prediction against the symbolic formula and revise any mismatch.

Support each conclusion with the numerical, graphical, FTC, or strategy evidence requested.

Expected evidence: A valid analysis uses $A'=f$ and $A''=f'$, identifies changes at the zeros of $f$, and confirms the symbolic accumulation formula.
Scoring criteria: Full credit requires linked graphical/symbolic evidence, FTC-based predictions, checks against a formula, and documented revision if needed.
Estimated student time: 25 minutes
Answer: A valid analysis uses $A'=f$ and $A''=f'$, identifies changes at the zeros of $f$, and confirms the symbolic accumulation formula.
Solution / scoring notes: Full credit requires linked graphical/symbolic evidence, FTC-based predictions, checks against a formula, and documented revision if needed.

Section 6.4 - FTC Part 2 and Antiderivatives

U6-S4-PERF01
Section 6.4 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Performance / Future Task

Audit these four claims and repair every incorrect one: (1) $\int 3x^2\,dx=x^3$; (2) $\int_0^2 3x^2\,dx=8+C$; (3) if $F'=f$, then $\int_a^b f=F(a)-F(b)$; (4) all antiderivatives of the same continuous function differ by a constant. Build a short decision guide explaining when $C$ belongs and how bounds affect evaluation.

Support each conclusion with the numerical, graphical, FTC, or strategy evidence requested.

Expected evidence: Claims 1 and 4 need the antiderivative-family distinction; claim 2 must omit $C$; claim 3 must reverse the subtraction. The guide must distinguish definite values from indefinite families.
Scoring criteria: Score for precise notation, repaired claims, and a coherent decision guide rather than isolated corrections.
Estimated student time: 15-20 minutes
Answer: Claims 1 and 4 need the antiderivative-family distinction; claim 2 must omit $C$; claim 3 must reverse the subtraction. The guide must distinguish definite values from indefinite families.
Solution / scoring notes: Score for precise notation, repaired claims, and a coherent decision guide rather than isolated corrections.

Section 6.5 - u-Substitution and Strategy

U6-S5-PERF01
Section 6.5 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Develop and test an integration-strategy guide using these integrands: $6x(3x^2+1)^4$, $(x^3+2x)/x$, $(x^2+3x+5)/(x+1)$, and $1/(x^2+4x+5)$. For each, choose the first move (substitution, rewriting, division, or completing the square), carry the integral through, explain why the chosen first move exposes the structure, and revise your guide if one rule fails on another integrand.

Support each conclusion with the numerical, graphical, FTC, or strategy evidence requested.

Expected evidence: A complete guide maps the four structures to substitution, algebraic simplification, polynomial division, and completing the square, with correct antiderivatives.
Scoring criteria: Scoring emphasizes strategic classification, successful execution, explanation of structure, and evidence of testing/revision.
Estimated student time: 25-30 minutes
Answer: A complete guide maps the four structures to substitution, algebraic simplification, polynomial division, and completing the square, with correct antiderivatives.
Solution / scoring notes: Scoring emphasizes strategic classification, successful execution, explanation of structure, and evidence of testing/revision.

U6-FA-D2-02-V4
Section 6.3 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Accumulation Function Analysis

A student says that for $F(x)=\int_0^x f(t)\,dt$, the value $F(3)$ must equal $f(3)$. Explain why this confuses two different quantities.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: $F(3)=\int_0^3 f(t)\,dt$ while $f(3)$ is the integrand value at one input.
Solution / scoring notes: FTC relates $F'(x)$ to $f(x)$; it does not generally equate the accumulation value with the integrand value.

DOK 3 - FTC Reasoning Critique

U6-FA-D3-03-V1
Section 6.4 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

FTC Reasoning Critique

Critique the statement $\int f=F+C$ and therefore $\int_a^b f=F(b)-F(a)+C$ and repair it with correct FTC/integral notation.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: The constant belongs to the indefinite family, but a definite evaluation has no appended $C$.
Solution / scoring notes: The repair should explicitly separate definite values from antiderivative-family notation.
U6-FA-D3-03-V2
Section 6.4 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

FTC Reasoning Critique

Critique the statement $\int_b^a f=\int_a^b f$ and repair it with correct FTC/integral notation.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Reversing bounds changes the sign.
Solution / scoring notes: The repair should explicitly separate definite values from antiderivative-family notation.
U6-FA-D3-03-V3
Section 6.4 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

FTC Reasoning Critique

Critique the statement $\int f\,dx$ is a single function rather than a family and repair it with correct FTC/integral notation.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Indefinite integration gives a family of antiderivatives differing by constants.
Solution / scoring notes: The repair should explicitly separate definite values from antiderivative-family notation.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Equal bounds give zero.
Solution / scoring notes: The repair should explicitly separate definite values from antiderivative-family notation.

Riemann error study: for $f(x)=x^2+1$ on $[0,4]$, compute left, right, midpoint, and trapezoid sums using 4 equal subintervals. Compare each with the exact integral, formulate method-error claims, then test those claims on a decreasing or concave-down function and revise any overgeneralization.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Distinguish a function value from a rate or slope in the final wording.

Answer: The response should distinguish monotonicity-driven left/right error from concavity-driven midpoint/trapezoid behavior and revise claims after the test case.
Solution / scoring notes: Score all four approximations, exact comparison, a tested claim, and evidence-based revision.

U6-SUM-V1-Q01
Section 6.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 6 Summative - Version 1

If $\int_0^2 f=-4$ and $\int_2^5 f=6$, what is $\int_0^5 f$?
  1. $2$
  2. $-10$
  3. $10$
  4. $-2$

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: A. $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use additivity and preserve signs.
U6-SUM-V1-Q02
Section 6.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 6 Summative - Version 1

For a positive increasing function, a right Riemann sum is expected to be which relative to the exact integral?
  1. always exact
  2. overestimate
  3. underestimate
  4. not related to monotonicity

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: B. overestimate
Solution / scoring notes: Compare the sample-point rectangle heights with the function on each subinterval.
U6-SUM-V1-Q03
Section 6.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 6 Summative - Version 1

If $F(x)=\int_0^{x^4}(t^2+1)\,dt$, what is $F'(x)$?
  1. $x^{8} + 1$
  2. $\frac{x^{8} + 1}{4 x^{3}}$
  3. $4 x^{3} \left(x^{8} + 1\right)$
  4. $4 x^{3}$

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: C. $4 x^{3} \left(x^{8} + 1\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 requires both upper-limit substitution and the chain factor.
U6-SUM-V1-Q04
Section 6.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 6 Summative - Version 1

Which is a general antiderivative of $3 x^{2}$?
  1. $x^{3}$
  2. $6 x+C$
  3. $3 x^{3}+C$
  4. $x^{3}+C$

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: D. $x^{3}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Reverse the power rule and include the constant for an indefinite integral.

Evaluate $\int_{0}^{3}(3 x^{2} - 2 x + 1)\,dx$ and explain why no constant of integration appears in the final definite value.

U6-SUM-V1-Q15

Evaluate $\int \frac{1}{x^{2} + 6 x + 10}\,dx$ by preparing the quadratic denominator.

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: $\operatorname{atan}{\left(x + 3 \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Complete the square to create a shifted $a^2+u^2$ form, then use the inverse-tangent antiderivative.
U6-SUM-V1-Q16

Unit 6 Summative - Version 1

The figure shows a piecewise-linear rate $R(t)$. Compute the net accumulated change from $t=0$ to $t=6$ and interpret the sign in context.

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: $-13$
Solution / scoring notes: Add signed trapezoid areas. A positive result means net increase; a negative result means net decrease.

Unit 6 Summative - Version 1

Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.

Unit 6 Summative - Version 4

Evaluate $\int 2 \left(2 x + 1\right)^{3}\,dx$.

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Answer: $\frac{\left(2 x + 1\right)^{4}}{4}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Let $u=2 x + 1$; then $du=2\,dx$, so the integral becomes a power of $u$.

If $F(x)=\int_0^{x^3}(t^2+1)\,dt$, what is $F'(x)$?

  2. $3 x^{2}$
  3. $\frac{x^{6} + 1}{3 x^{2}}$
  4. $3 x^{2} \left(x^{6} + 1\right)$

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Keep the final response to one clear mathematical statement.

Answer: D. $3 x^{2} \left(x^{6} + 1\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: FTC Part 1 requires both upper-limit substitution and the chain factor.

  2. $x^{5}$

  4. $5 x^{5}+C$

Answer: A. $x^{5}+C$

Unit 6 Summative - Version 7

Evaluate $\int \frac{x^2+3x+5}{x+1}\,dx$ using an efficient algebraic step first.

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

Place the requested quantity first in the final response.

Answer: $\frac{x^{2}}{2} + 2 x + 3 \log{\left(\left|{x + 1}\right| \right)}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomial long division gives $\frac{\left(x + 1\right) \left(x + 2\right) + 3}{x + 1}$ before integrating.

Unit 6 Summative - Version 8

For the integrand $\frac{x^3+1}{x^2+1}$, compare substitution with algebraic division as first strategies. Choose the more efficient first move and justify it.

Use AP-calculus notation, exact values when appropriate, and contextual units or justification when requested.

If a value is an estimate, label it as an estimate rather than an equality.

Answer: Polynomial division is the better first move because the numerator degree is at least the denominator degree; it exposes a simpler rational remainder.
Solution / scoring notes: Strategic integration begins by reading the integrand structure rather than automatically choosing $u$.
